CRITICAL beh.class_conformance — ddos/DDoS-LOIC-UDP misses 2 of 3 behavioural expectation(s), worst by 85.3%

The flows carrying this label do not behave the way the label names. Either the window is over the wrong traffic, or the band in expectations.yaml is wrong -- the measured rate and the floor it missed are both reported so the reader can tell which.

SELECT count(*) AS flows,
       sum(CASE WHEN (coalesce(proto, '') = 'udp') THEN 1 ELSE 0 END) AS udp_transport,
       approx_count_distinct("id.orig_h") AS distinct_orig_h,
       approx_count_distinct("id.resp_h") AS distinct_resp_h,
       approx_count_distinct("id.orig_p") AS distinct_orig_p,
       approx_count_distinct("id.resp_p") AS distinct_resp_p
FROM read_parquet('s3://ids-datalakec48eb2cab942494ba5059fac3b3527d9/labelled/cic-ids-2018/**/*.parquet', union_by_name=true)
WHERE label_class = 'ddos' AND label_raw = 'DDoS-LOIC-UDP'
CRITICAL beh.executed_ratio — FTP-BruteForce: 100.0% of flows never sent a byte (192,294 of 192,294)

FTP-BruteForce cannot execute without the attacker sending payload, so these flows are connection attempts labelled as the attack itself. They inflate brute_force and, because they are trivially separable from real attack traffic, they inflate any accuracy figure measured on it.

CRITICAL beh.port_anomaly — DoS-SlowHTTPTest: modal destination port is 21 (100.0% of flows), not 80/443/8080

The window labelled DoS-SlowHTTPTest sits on a service its class cannot plausibly be attacking. Either the schedule's endpoints or times are wrong for this attack, or the attack is misnamed in the manifest -- in both cases the flows under this label are not the attack the label describes.

CRITICAL cor.analyzer_presence — 1 attack window(s) require a log the lake does not have (ftp)

Zeek writes an application log only once it has parsed that application. These attacks are named for a protocol whose log was never produced anywhere in the dataset, so the traffic the schedule calls an attack contained none of the protocol the attack is made of. The flows are connection attempts, or the window is wrong.

CRITICAL int.bytes_physically_possible — 1,753,011 flow(s) report more payload bytes than wire bytes across 2 class(es); benign, botnet materially affected

Payload bytes are a subset of the bytes that crossed the wire, so a flow where orig_bytes exceeds orig_ip_bytes (or resp_bytes exceeds resp_ip_bytes) is arithmetically impossible: Zeek derives the first from TCP sequence numbers, which wrap on scan and RST traffic, and counts the second off the wire, which cannot. These are the same columns that carry the largest label correlation in 2017 (point-biserial -0.442) and the largest attack-class mutual information (0.835), so the corruption sits exactly where the label signal is and every expectation or model built on it inherits the wrap. Use orig_ip_bytes and resp_ip_bytes instead.

    FROM read_parquet('s3://ids-datalakec48eb2cab942494ba5059fac3b3527d9/labelled/cic-ids-2018/**/*.parquet', union_by_name = true)
    GROUP BY label_class ORDER BY 2 DESC
CRITICAL int.capture_spans_disjoint — 3 capture(s) begin at an identical timestamp

Captures sharing a first timestamp to the millisecond did not begin at the same moment by chance -- they contain the same traffic. The flows are counted more than once in every class total, and because the labelling predicate has no capture term, one schedule window labels all the copies. This is the strongest evidence that the capture folders are not the independent recordings their names claim.

Reproduce

SELECT capture, count(*) AS n, min(ts) AS ts_min, max(ts) AS ts_max
    FROM read_parquet('s3://ids-datalakec48eb2cab942494ba5059fac3b3527d9/labelled/cic-ids-2018/**/*.parquet', union_by_name = true)
    WHERE ts IS NOT NULL
    GROUP BY capture ORDER BY 3
CRITICAL int.capture_spans_disjoint — 5 capture pair(s) overlap in wall-clock time, the worst by 59.36 hours

Labelling matches on time and endpoints only, so a window selects a wall-clock interval across every capture at once. Where two captures cover the same second, a rule written for one of them silently claims traffic from the other, and no capture-level correction can separate them afterwards. It also rules out `capture` as a split key: the overlapping traffic would appear in both train and test.

    GROUP BY capture ORDER BY 3
CRITICAL xds.benign_baseline — cic-ddos-2019's benign class is closer to its own 'ddos' (JS 0.106) than to cic-ids-2018's benign (JS 0.450)

Benign traffic from two testbeds should resemble each other more than either resembles an attack. cic-ddos-2019's does not, which means its benign class is largely unlabelled attack traffic. It cannot serve as a false-positive baseline, and any accuracy computed against it is measuring contamination.

WITH rules AS (SELECT * FROM (VALUES
        (10, 'BruteForce-Web', 'web_attack', 1519309020.0, 1519313100.0, ['18.218.115.60', '172.31.69.28', '18.218.83.150'], CAST(NULL AS VARCHAR)),
        (11, 'BruteForce-XSS', 'web_attack', 1519321800.0, 1519324200.0, ['18.218.115.60', '172.31.69.28', '18.218.83.150'], CAST(NULL AS VARCHAR)),
        (12, 'SQL-Injection', 'web_attack', 1519330500.0, 1519331400.0, ['18.218.115.60', '172.31.69.28', '18.218.83.150'], CAST(NULL AS VARCHAR)),
        (13, 'BruteForce-Web', 'web_attack', 1519394580.0, 1519398240.0, ['18.218.115.60', '172.31.69.28', '18.218.83.150'], CAST(NULL AS VARCHAR)),
        (14, 'BruteForce-XSS', 'web_attack', 1519405200.0, 1519409460.0, ['18.218.115.60', '172.31.69.28', '18.218.83.150'], CAST(NULL AS VARCHAR)),
        (15, 'SQL-Injection', 'web_attack', 1519412700.0, 1519413540.0, ['18.218.115.60', '172.31.69.28', '18.218.83.150'], CAST(NULL AS VARCHAR))
      ) AS t(rid, rname, rclass, t0, t1, eps, vpfx)),
      ev AS (SELECT "ts", "uid", "id.orig_h", "id.resp_h", "uri", "method", "status_code" FROM read_parquet('s3://ids-datalakec48eb2cab942494ba5059fac3b3527d9/parquets/cic-ids-2018/**/http.parquet', union_by_name=true)
        WHERE ts IS NOT NULL AND ts >= 1519309020.0 AND ts < 1519413540.0)
      SELECT r.rid, r.rname, count(*) AS records,
             count(DISTINCT e."uid") AS uids,
             sum(CASE WHEN regexp_matches(lower(coalesce(e."uri", '')), '(?:union[^a-z0-9]{1,12}select)|(?:select[^;]{1,80}from)|(?:(?:''|%27)[^a-z0-9]{0,8}(?:or|and)[^a-z0-9]{1,8}(?:''|%27)?[0-9]{1,3}[^a-z0-9]{0,4}=)|(?:\bor[^a-z0-9]{1,4}1[^a-z0-9]{0,4}=[^a-z0-9]{0,4}1)|(?:information_schema|table_schema)|(?:(?:sleep|benchmark|waitfor[^a-z]{1,6}delay)[^a-z]{0,4}\()|(?:group_concat|concat_ws|xp_cmdshell|load_file)') THEN 1 ELSE 0 END) AS sqli,
             sum(CASE WHEN regexp_matches(lower(coalesce(e."uri", '')), '(?:<script|%3cscript|&lt;script)|(?:</script|%3c%2fscript)|(?:javascript[:%])|(?:on(?:error|load|mouseover|focus|click)[^a-z]{0,6}=)|(?:(?:alert|prompt|confirm)[^a-z]{0,4}\()|(?:document[^a-z]{0,4}\.[^a-z]{0,4}cookie)|(?:<(?:img|svg|iframe|body)[^a-z]|%3c(?:img|svg|iframe|body))') THEN 1 ELSE 0 END) AS xss,
             count(DISTINCT CASE WHEN regexp_matches(lower(coalesce(e."uri", '')), '(?:union[^a-z0-9]{1,12}select)|(?:select[^;]{1,80}from)|(?:(?:''|%27)[^a-z0-9]{0,8}(?:or|and)[^a-z0-9]{1,8}(?:''|%27)?[0-9]{1,3}[^a-z0-9]{0,4}=)|(?:\bor[^a-z0-9]{1,4}1[^a-z0-9]{0,4}=[^a-z0-9]{0,4}1)|(?:information_schema|table_schema)|(?:(?:sleep|benchmark|waitfor[^a-z]{1,6}delay)[^a-z]{0,4}\()|(?:group_concat|concat_ws|xp_cmdshell|load_file)') OR regexp_matches(lower(coalesce(e."uri", '')), '(?:<script|%3cscript|&lt;script)|(?:</script|%3c%2fscript)|(?:javascript[:%])|(?:on(?:error|load|mouseover|focus|click)[^a-z]{0,6}=)|(?:(?:alert|prompt|confirm)[^a-z]{0,4}\()|(?:document[^a-z]{0,4}\.[^a-z]{0,4}cookie)|(?:<(?:img|svg|iframe|body)[^a-z]|%3c(?:img|svg|iframe|body))') THEN e."uid" END) AS uids_sig,
             sum(CASE WHEN upper(coalesce(e."method", '')) = 'GET' THEN 1 ELSE 0 END) AS gets,
             sum(CASE WHEN upper(coalesce(e."method", '')) = 'POST' THEN 1 ELSE 0 END) AS posts,
             sum(CASE WHEN (regexp_matches(lower(coalesce(e."uri", '')), '(?:union[^a-z0-9]{1,12}select)|(?:select[^;]{1,80}from)|(?:(?:''|%27)[^a-z0-9]{0,8}(?:or|and)[^a-z0-9]{1,8}(?:''|%27)?[0-9]{1,3}[^a-z0-9]{0,4}=)|(?:\bor[^a-z0-9]{1,4}1[^a-z0-9]{0,4}=[^a-z0-9]{0,4}1)|(?:information_schema|table_schema)|(?:(?:sleep|benchmark|waitfor[^a-z]{1,6}delay)[^a-z]{0,4}\()|(?:group_concat|concat_ws|xp_cmdshell|load_file)') OR regexp_matches(lower(coalesce(e."uri", '')), '(?:<script|%3cscript|&lt;script)|(?:</script|%3c%2fscript)|(?:javascript[:%])|(?:on(?:error|load|mouseover|focus|click)[^a-z]{0,6}=)|(?:(?:alert|prompt|confirm)[^a-z]{0,4}\()|(?:document[^a-z]{0,4}\.[^a-z]{0,4}cookie)|(?:<(?:img|svg|iframe|body)[^a-z]|%3c(?:img|svg|iframe|body))')) AND e."status_code" BETWEEN 200 AND 299
                      THEN 1 ELSE 0 END) AS sig_2xx
      FROM rules r JOIN ev e ON e.ts >= r.t0 AND e.ts < r.t1
          AND (coalesce(list_contains(r.eps, e."id.orig_h"), false)
            OR coalesce(list_contains(r.eps, e."id.resp_h"), false)
            OR coalesce(starts_with(e."id.orig_h", r.vpfx), false)
            OR coalesce(starts_with(e."id.resp_h", r.vpfx), false)
            OR (r.eps IS NULL AND r.vpfx IS NULL))
      GROUP BY r.rid, r.rname ORDER BY r.rid
MAJOR int.capture_spans_disjoint — 4 capture(s) span longer than 24 hours

A capture named for a single day should span well under a day. A longer span means two recordings merged under one folder, a stray flow carrying a timestamp from elsewhere, or a conversion that did not preserve the clock -- and a span that wide is what makes captures overlap each other in the first place.

MAJOR int.null_not_zero — duration is null for 6.91% of benign flows

4,055,142 flow(s) carry no duration. The EDA spec coalesces this feature's nulls to 0, so those flows are indistinguishable from flows that genuinely measured zero, and a class whose zero mass is really missing data will look flood-shaped whether or not it is. Nothing here says the labels are wrong -- it constrains which features a downstream claim about the affected classes may rest on, and requires that nulls be excluded explicitly rather than read as zeros.

MAJOR orc.missed_attacks — 46,455 benign flow(s) are attacks according to the external oracle

These flows carry no rule match, so they are benign only because the published schedule does not mention them -- and an independent manual re-labelling of the same packets says an attack was running. Every one is currently training data for the benign class. A further 1,084 flow(s) match only `- Attempted` oracle rows and are excluded from the count above: an attempt that delivered nothing is a defensible thing to call benign.

MAJOR qtn.overlap_with_attack — 4 quarantine region(s) overlap 4 scheduled attack window(s) in both time and endpoints

The schedule claims infiltration against these hosts in these minutes while the manifest declines to adjudicate the same flows. Both cannot stand: a window is either evidence of what happened there or it is not. Read each region's justification below and either narrow the region to the span the schedule does not cover, or withdraw the window.

3unlabellable-second-stage2018-03-01 14:00-15:3719Infiltrationinfiltration2018-03-01 14:00-15:375,8801172.31.69.13, 18.216.254.154CIC describes Infiltration as two stages — "First level: Dropbox download in a windows machine. Second Level: Nmap and portscan" — and Table 2 publishes only the first. It names the infected host in the Victim column (172.31.69.24 on 28-02, 172.31.69.13 on 01-03), but never the scan targets and never a separate window for the second stage. The internal scan is therefore unlabellable from the schedule: we cannot say which flows it produced, so every one of them falls to benign by the closed-world assumption. That default is not neutral here. The attack rules match 8 flows across roughly 4.5 scheduled hours, while these windows carry on the order of 175,000 unmatched flows per 10-minute bucket — a benign class certified by nothing but the absence of a published target list. The 8 matched flows are no better founded than the unmatched ones: none of them completed a connection (conn_state OTH 50% / S1 37.5% / RSTR 12.5%) and the longest runs 13,254.7 s against a 5,820 s window, a 2.28x overrun, so they are mid-stream traffic caught by a time window rather than an observed attack (B11). They sit inside these regions and are quarantined with everything else; `infiltration` therefore carries no adjudicable evidence in this dataset and must not be evaluated as a class (B10). DistriNet derives the two phases as running to 18:39 and 19:38, far past CIC's published 14:40 and 15:37, so even as declared these regions UNDER-cover the phase (A3). They are drawn at the published times because those are what the source states; the DistriNet finding is why the region cannot be read as an upper bound on the contamination. Widening them, or replacing them with real second-stage attack rules, is DEFERRED until sch.window_sweep has measured where this traffic actually stops in our own lake: the DistriNet end times are a third party's derivation and are recorded as the hypothesis that sweep should test, never as a transcribed fact. Scoped to the hosts CIC does name, so the rest of each day — which the schedule describes normally — stays adjudicated. These regions coincide with the four scheduled Infiltration windows by construction, so qtn.overlap_with_attack reports them: we are declining to adjudicate a span the schedule claims. That contradiction is real and this is its answer — the schedule's claim here amounts to 8 unfinished flows and accounts for none of the rest.

SELECT label_class, count(*) AS flows,
           max(duration) AS duration_max,
           approx_quantile(duration, 0.99) AS duration_p99,
           count(*) FILTER (WHERE duration > CASE label_class WHEN 'brute_force' THEN 5880.0 WHEN 'dos' THEN 3420.0 WHEN 'ddos' THEN 3960.0 WHEN 'web_attack' THEN 4260.0 WHEN 'infiltration' THEN 5880.0 WHEN 'botnet' THEN 5520.0 END) AS overrunning,
           histogram(conn_state) FILTER (WHERE duration > CASE label_class WHEN 'brute_force' THEN 5880.0 WHEN 'dos' THEN 3420.0 WHEN 'ddos' THEN 3960.0 WHEN 'web_attack' THEN 4260.0 WHEN 'infiltration' THEN 5880.0 WHEN 'botnet' THEN 5520.0 END) AS states
    FROM read_parquet('s3://ids-datalakec48eb2cab942494ba5059fac3b3527d9/labelled/cic-ids-2018/**/*.parquet', union_by_name = true)
    WHERE label_class IN ('brute_force', 'dos', 'ddos', 'web_attack', 'infiltration', 'botnet') AND duration IS NOT NULL
    GROUP BY 1 ORDER BY 3 DESC

      FROM conn c JOIN rules r ON c.ts >= r.t0 AND c.ts < r.t1)

           sum(CASE WHEN endpoint_match THEN 1 ELSE 0 END) AS matched

        AND (list_contains(r.vic, c."id.orig_h") OR list_contains(r.vic, c."id.resp_h")))
  ) AS endpoint_match
      FROM conn c JOIN rules r ON c.ts >= r.t0 AND c.ts < r.t1)

           count(*) AS in_window,
           sum(CASE WHEN endpoint_match THEN 1 ELSE 0 END) AS matched

      FROM conn c JOIN rules r ON c.ts >= r.t0 AND c.ts < r.t1)

           count(*) AS in_window,
           sum(CASE WHEN endpoint_match THEN 1 ELSE 0 END) AS matched

      FROM conn c JOIN rules r ON c.ts >= r.t0 AND c.ts < r.t1)

           count(*) AS in_window,
           sum(CASE WHEN endpoint_match THEN 1 ELSE 0 END) AS matched

    conn AS (SELECT * FROM read_parquet('s3://ids-datalakec48eb2cab942494ba5059fac3b3527d9/labelled/cic-ids-2018/**/*.parquet', union_by_name = true)),
    inwin AS (
      SELECT r.rid, r.rname, c."id.orig_h" AS orig, c."id.resp_h" AS resp,
             (
    (r.atk IS NOT NULL AND (
        (list_contains(r.atk, c."id.orig_h") AND (
        (r.vic IS NULL AND r.vpfx IS NULL)
        OR coalesce(list_contains(r.vic, c."id.resp_h"), false)
        OR coalesce(starts_with(c."id.resp_h", r.vpfx), false)
      ))
     OR (list_contains(r.atk, c."id.resp_h") AND (
        (r.vic IS NULL AND r.vpfx IS NULL)
        OR coalesce(list_contains(r.vic, c."id.orig_h"), false)
        OR coalesce(starts_with(c."id.orig_h", r.vpfx), false)
      ))
    ))
    OR (r.atk IS NULL AND r.vic IS NOT NULL
        AND (list_contains(r.vic, c."id.orig_h") OR list_contains(r.vic, c."id.resp_h")))
  ) AS endpoint_match
      FROM conn c JOIN rules r ON c.ts >= r.t0 AND c.ts < r.t1)
    SELECT rid, rname,
           count(*) AS in_window,
           sum(CASE WHEN endpoint_match THEN 1 ELSE 0 END) AS matched
    FROM inwin GROUP BY 1, 2 ORDER BY in_window DESC

    GROUP BY 1, 2

MAJOR xds.class_coheres — 'botnet' differs more between cic-ids-2017 and cic-ids-2018 (JS 0.300) than 'ddos' and 'dos' differ inside one dataset (JS 0.153)

A canonical class should look more like itself in another testbed than like a different attack in the same testbed. 'botnet' does not: it spans 2.0x the distance that separates two genuinely different classes. Whatever a cross-domain result about 'botnet' measures, it is not one phenomenon.

MAJOR xds.class_support_matrix — 5 class(es) lack support in 2+ datasets and cannot carry a cross-domain claim

A class needs at least 1,000 flows in at least 2 datasets before a transfer result about it means anything. These do not have it, so they should be dropped, folded into a broader class, or reported as single-domain observations -- not evaluated as transfer.

MINOR con.duplicate_conflict — 50,675 flow(s) are provably mislabelled (0.0813%): identical flows carrying different classes

32,347 distinct feature vectors appear in the labelled zone under more than one class. The vectors are built from every observable conn column with identity and timing removed, so the flows in a group are indistinguishable to any model. Taking the majority label of each group as correct, 50,675 flows are labelled wrongly -- a lower bound, since a whole group could be wrong together. Empty and near-empty flows dominate these groups in practice, which is the signature of a time-window label catching connection attempts that carry no attack.

WITH vec AS (
    SELECT "id.orig_p" AS k0, "id.resp_p" AS k1, "proto" AS k2, "duration" AS k3, "orig_bytes" AS k4, "resp_bytes" AS k5, "conn_state" AS k6, "local_orig" AS k7, "local_resp" AS k8, "missed_bytes" AS k9, "history" AS k10, "orig_pkts" AS k11, "orig_ip_bytes" AS k12, "resp_pkts" AS k13, "resp_ip_bytes" AS k14, "ip_proto" AS k15, "service" AS k16, "tunnel_parents" AS k17, label_class, count(*) AS n
    FROM read_parquet('s3://ids-datalakec48eb2cab942494ba5059fac3b3527d9/labelled/cic-ids-2018/**/*.parquet', union_by_name=true)
    GROUP BY ALL
), grp AS (
    SELECT *,
           sum(n)   OVER w AS grp_flows,
           count(*) OVER w AS grp_classes,
           row_number() OVER (PARTITION BY k0, k1, k2, k3, k4, k5, k6, k7, k8, k9, k10, k11, k12, k13, k14, k15, k16, k17
                              ORDER BY n DESC, label_class) AS rk
    FROM vec WINDOW w AS (PARTITION BY k0, k1, k2, k3, k4, k5, k6, k7, k8, k9, k10, k11, k12, k13, k14, k15, k16, k17)
)
SELECT * FROM grp WHERE grp_classes > 1
MINOR int.null_not_zero — service is null for 100.00% of infiltration flows and 3 other class(es)

8,368,457 flow(s) carry no service. Nulls in this feature stay visible as their own level rather than collapsing onto a real value, but they are still absent evidence rather than a measurement. Nothing here says the labels are wrong -- it constrains which features a downstream claim about the affected classes may rest on, and requires that nulls be excluded explicitly rather than read as zeros.

SELECT label_class, count(*), sum(CASE WHEN "service" IS NULL THEN 1 ELSE 0 END) FROM read_parquet('s3://ids-datalakec48eb2cab942494ba5059fac3b3527d9/labelled/cic-ids-2018/**/*.parquet') GROUP BY 1
MINOR orc.attempted_axis — 304,857 of 3,698,919 matched attack flow(s) (8.24%) are `- Attempted` in the external oracle

The oracle records, per flow, whether an attack actually executed and why not. Our labels have no such axis, so this is a gap in our label model rather than an error in it -- nothing here says a label is wrong, and no disagreement involving this axis is counted against us anywhere in this tier. What it constrains is what an attack class may be quoted as evidence OF: a class whose matched flows are largely attempts is a class of connection attempts, and a flow count taken from it is not a count of executed attacks. 2 class(es) are above 25%.

MINOR qtn.coverage — infiltration: 100.0% of the class is quarantined, leaving 0 adjudicable flow(s)

Most of this class sits inside a declared region, so what is left is not a sample of the class -- it is the part the quarantine did not reach. Any per-class figure computed from the remainder describes that residue and nothing wider. There is no adjudicable evidence left at all, so the class cannot be evaluated and should be dropped rather than reported.

MINOR sch.interval_overlap_delta — 21 flow(s) (0.000%) change label when matched by interval overlap instead of start time

A flow is currently an attack only if it STARTED inside the window. Matching on overlap instead -- the flow was alive at some point during the attack -- moves these flows. Slow-DoS attacks hold connections open by design, so for those the start-only rule systematically under-labels.

    (21, 'Bot', 'botnet', 1520015040.0, 1520020560.0, ['18.219.211.138'], ['172.31.69.23', '18.217.218.111', '172.31.69.17', '18.222.10.237', '172.31.69.14', '18.222.86.193', '172.31.69.12', '18.222.62.221', '172.31.69.10', '13.59.9.106', '172.31.69.8', '18.222.102.2', '172.31.69.6', '18.219.212.0', '172.31.69.26', '18.216.105.13', '172.31.69.29', '18.219.163.126', '172.31.69.30', '18.216.164.12'], CAST(NULL AS VARCHAR), true)
  ) AS r(rid, rname, rclass, t0, t1, atk, vic, vpfx, needs_payload)),
    conn AS (SELECT * FROM read_parquet('s3://ids-datalakec48eb2cab942494ba5059fac3b3527d9/labelled/cic-ids-2018/**/*.parquet', union_by_name = true)),
    ov AS (
      SELECT c.uid, min(r.rid) AS rid
      FROM conn c JOIN rules r
        ON c.ts < r.t1 AND (c.ts + coalesce(c.duration, 0)) >= r.t0 AND (
    (r.atk IS NOT NULL AND (
        (list_contains(r.atk, c."id.orig_h") AND (
        (r.vic IS NULL AND r.vpfx IS NULL)
        OR coalesce(list_contains(r.vic, c."id.resp_h"), false)
        OR coalesce(starts_with(c."id.resp_h", r.vpfx), false)
      ))
     OR (list_contains(r.atk, c."id.resp_h") AND (
        (r.vic IS NULL AND r.vpfx IS NULL)
        OR coalesce(list_contains(r.vic, c."id.orig_h"), false)
        OR coalesce(starts_with(c."id.orig_h", r.vpfx), false)
      ))
    ))
    OR (r.atk IS NULL AND r.vic IS NOT NULL
        AND (list_contains(r.vic, c."id.orig_h") OR list_contains(r.vic, c."id.resp_h")))
  )
      GROUP BY c.uid)
    SELECT c.label_class, r.rclass AS overlap_class,
           count(*) AS n, sum(coalesce(c.duration, 0)) AS total_duration
    FROM conn c LEFT JOIN ov USING (uid)
    LEFT JOIN rules r ON r.rid = ov.rid
    WHERE coalesce(r.rclass, 'benign') <> c.label_class
    GROUP BY 1, 2 ORDER BY n DESC

    SELECT r.rid,

        (r.vic IS NULL AND r.vpfx IS NULL)

INFO art.cicflowmeter_artefacts — CICFlowMeter artefact probes ran over 62,341,777 flows and found none of the signatures

Reported because the negative result is the finding. Rosay et al. (ICISSP 2022) identified four artefact classes that CICFlowMeter writes into every CSV it generates -- constant bulk features from an integer division, ARP frames read as IPv4 traffic between 8.0.6.4 and 8.6.0.1, non-TCP/UDP packets collapsed to protocol 0 with ports 0, and TCP flows cut at the first FIN whose halves can end up labelled differently -- and stated that every dataset shipping CICFlowMeter CSVs carries them, CIC-IDS-2017, CSE-CIC-IDS-2018 and CIC-DDoS-2019 included. Talos does not use those CSVs; the lake is Zeek output over the original pcaps. This check is the measurement behind that claim, so that 'our features do not carry the published artefacts' is a reading rather than an assertion. Probe 1 has no literal equivalent in Zeek's schema and is reported as inapplicable rather than faked.

SELECT count(*) AS groups, coalesce(sum(n), 0) AS flows FROM (
            SELECT "id.orig_h", "id.orig_p", "id.resp_h", "id.resp_p", ts, count(*) AS n
            FROM read_parquet('s3://ids-datalakec48eb2cab942494ba5059fac3b3527d9/labelled/cic-ids-2018/**/*.parquet', union_by_name = true)
            WHERE (proto = 'tcp' AND history IS NOT NULL AND contains(history, 'F') AND contains(history, 'f'))
            GROUP BY "id.orig_h", "id.orig_p", "id.resp_h", "id.resp_p", ts
            HAVING count(DISTINCT label_binary) > 1)

        (1, 'SSH-Bruteforce', 'brute_force', 1518631260.0, 1518636720.0, ['172.31.70.6', '13.58.98.64', '172.31.69.25', '18.217.21.148'], CAST(NULL AS VARCHAR))
      ) AS t(rid, rname, rclass, t0, t1, eps, vpfx)),
          ev AS (SELECT "ts", "uid", "id.orig_h", "id.resp_h", "auth_attempts", "auth_success", "client" FROM read_parquet('s3://ids-datalakec48eb2cab942494ba5059fac3b3527d9/parquets/cic-ids-2018/**/ssh.parquet', union_by_name=true)
        WHERE ts IS NOT NULL AND ts >= 1518631260.0 AND ts < 1518636720.0)

                 count(DISTINCT e."uid") AS uids,
                 sum(coalesce(e."auth_attempts", 0)) AS attempts,
                 max(e."auth_attempts") AS max_attempts, sum(CASE WHEN e."auth_success" THEN 1 ELSE 0 END) AS successes,
                 count(DISTINCT e."id.orig_h") AS sources
          FROM rules r JOIN ev e ON e.ts >= r.t0 AND e.ts < r.t1

            OR coalesce(starts_with(e."id.resp_h", r.vpfx), false)

          GROUP BY r.rid ORDER BY r.rid

        WHERE ts IS NOT NULL AND ts >= 1519309020.0 AND ts < 1519413540.0)
      SELECT r.rid, r.rname, count(*) AS records,

             sum(CASE WHEN upper(coalesce(e."method", '')) = 'POST' THEN 1 ELSE 0 END) AS posts,

                      THEN 1 ELSE 0 END) AS sig_2xx
      FROM rules r JOIN ev e ON e.ts >= r.t0 AND e.ts < r.t1
          AND (coalesce(list_contains(r.eps, e."id.orig_h"), false)

            OR coalesce(starts_with(e."id.resp_h", r.vpfx), false)
            OR (r.eps IS NULL AND r.vpfx IS NULL))

INFO orc.coverage — external oracle matches 93.44% of our flows (58,198,175 of 62,285,938)

Share of our flows for which some manually-forensicated oracle row overlaps in time on the same unordered 5-tuple. This number gates every other figure in this tier: an agreement rate computed over a small unrepresentative match set describes the match set, not the labels. Flow COUNTS are deliberately not compared -- CICFlowMeter's 120-second active timeout splits one Zeek connection into many oracle rows, which is what the fan-out columns show.

SELECT label_class, count(*), count(*) FILTER (WHERE oracle_rows > 0) FROM read_parquet('/home/ubuntu/talos/reports/validation/oracle_cic-ids-2018_uid.parquet') GROUP BY 1
INFO orc.direction_reversal — 18,774 of 58,198,175 oracle matches (0.03%) have the originator reversed

CICFlowMeter fixes direction from the first packet it sees and Zeek from the SYN, so the two disagree wherever a handshake is absent. The join keys on the unordered 5-tuple precisely so these flows are matched instead of lost, and this is the measurement that would otherwise be invisible. It indicts neither labelling. It does bound the rest of the tier -- agreement is computed over direction-agreeing matches only -- and it means any orig/resp-relative feature is not describing the same endpoint on both sides.

SELECT count(*) FILTER (WHERE oracle_rows_dir_agree > 0)::DOUBLE / count(*) FROM read_parquet('/home/ubuntu/talos/reports/validation/oracle_cic-ids-2018_uid.parquet') WHERE oracle_rows > 0
INFO orc.false_attacks — 30 attack flow(s) are benign according to the external oracle (0.00% of matched attack flows)

Our labels are a time window and a pair of addresses, so a window wider than the attack collects whatever else those hosts were doing. These flows are in an attack class and the independent re-labelling reads them as ordinary traffic. They inflate the class and credit any detector evaluated on them with finding something that was not there. 30 of these fall in windows CIC is known to have got wrong (DoS Slowhttptest absent 16-02), where the correction belongs in the manifest.

What ran, and what did not

A skipped check is not a passed check. Skipped and errored rows are shaded and flagged rather than listed alongside the passes, because "did not run" and "ran and found nothing" are different claims about this dataset, and a report that blurs them is worth less than no report.

Provenance

The manifest and taxonomy hashes are the ones the checks read. If they differ from the hashes stamped into the labelled zone, int.provenance_current says so above — and nothing else on this page can be trusted until it does.
